\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{2\frac{\log n}{(\log d)^{2}}}\left(\frac{ekd}{2}\right)\left(\frac{e^{2}d}{2}\right)^{k}\leq\frac{ed}{(\log d)^{2}}\ \frac{\log n}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{2\frac{\log n}{(\log d)^{2}}}\left(\frac{e^{2}d}{2}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n^{-9/10}\left({e^{2}d}/{2}\right)^{2\frac{\log n}{(\log d)^{2}}}\leq n^{-3/4}\kern 5.0pt,

where the last two inequalities hold for sufficiently large
d
.

A.2.
Standard Linear Algebra

Claim A.1
.

For any two
N\times N
symmetric matrices
\UpD,\UpY
, such that
\UpD
is non-singular,
we have
\|\UpD\cdot\UpY\cdot\UpD\|\leq\|\UpY\|\cdot\|\UpD^{2}\|
.

Proof of
A.1
.

We have

(A.1)

\displaystyle\|\UpD\cdot\UpY\cdot\UpD\|

\displaystyle=\|\UpD\cdot\UpY\cdot\UpD\cdot\overline{\UpD}\cdot\overline{\UpY}\cdot\overline{\UpD}\|^{1/2}\kern 5.0pt,

where for matrix
\UpA
, we let
\overline{A}
denote its transpose.

For any symmetric
N\times N
matrix
\UpB
we have

\|\UpB\|\leq\|\UpD^{-1}\cdot\UpB\cdot\UpD\|.

Recall that
\UpD
is non-singular, hence the above is well-defined.

Since the matrix inside the norm on the r.h.s. of (
A.1
)
is symmetric, we combine the above two relations and get

\displaystyle\|\UpD\cdot\UpY\cdot\UpD\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\UpY\cdot\UpD\cdot\overline{\UpD}\cdot\overline{\UpY}\cdot\overline{\UpD}\cdot\UpD\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\UpY\|^{1/2}\cdot\|\UpD\cdot\overline{\UpD}\|^{1/2}\cdot\|\overline{\UpY}\|^{1/2}\cdot\|\overline{\UpD}\cdot\UpD\|^{1/2}\leq\|\UpY\|\cdot\|\UpD^{2}\|\kern 5.0pt.

For the last inequality we use that our matrices are symmetric.
The claim follows.
∎

A.3.
Bound on maximum coupling value

Let
{\mathcal{H}}
be the event that there is an edge
e
in
\mathbold{G}(n,d/n)
such that
the coupling
|\mathbold{\gamma}_{e}|
is larger than
10\sqrt{\log n}

Lemma A.2
.

We have that
\Pr[{\mathcal{H}}]\leq n^{-3}
.

Proof.

For the standard normal random variable
\mathbold{\gamma}
, and every
t>0
, we have

(A.2)

\displaystyle\Pr[|\mathbold{\gamma}|>t]\leq\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}\cdot({1}/{t})\cdot e^{-t^{2}/2}\kern 5.74997pt.

Taking
t=10\sqrt{\log n}
, and large
n
, we have that

(A.3)

\displaystyle\Pr\left[|\mathbold{\gamma}|>10\sqrt{\log n}\right]\leq n^{-5}\kern 5.0pt.

The lemma follows from the union bound.
∎

A.4.
Tail Bound for sums of half-normal

We say that
Y
follows the
half-normal
distribution with parameter
\sigma
, if
Y=|X|
, where
X
follows the Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. For
N>0
integer, and
\sigma\geq 0
, let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{N}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be i.i.d standard Gaussians, and write

X=\sum\nolimits_{1\leq i\leq N}|X_{i}|\kern 5.0pt.

We show the following concentration bound for
X

Theorem A.3
.

For every
\delta\geq 0
, we have

(A.4)

\Pr\left[\,X>(1+\delta)\cdot\mathbb{E}[X]\,\right]\leq\exp\left(-N\cdot{\delta^{2}}/{\pi}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

For every real
t\geq 0
, consider the moment generating function
\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]
, as a non-negative random variable. Applying Markov’s inequality on
\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]
, we get that for every
a\in\mathbb{R}

(A.5)

\Pr\left[X>a\right]=\Pr\left[\exp(t\cdot X)>\exp(t\cdot a)\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]}{\exp(t\cdot a)}\kern 5.0pt.

To calculate
\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]
, we first observe that since
X_{i}
’s are i.i.d., so that

(A.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(t\cdot\sum\nolimits_{1\leq i\leq N}|X_{i}|\right)\right]=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(t\cdot|X_{1}|\right)\right]\right)^{N}\kern 5.0pt.

We now focus on
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(t\cdot|X_{1}|\right)\right]
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(t\cdot|X_{1}|\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\exp\left(t\cdot|x|\right)\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(tx-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=2\cdot\exp\left(\frac{t^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}\right)\cdot\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}+tx-\frac{x^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=2\cdot\exp\left(\frac{t^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}\right)\cdot\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\sigma\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{x}{\sigma}-\sigma t\right)^{2}\right)dx

(A.7)

\displaystyle=2\cdot\exp\left(\frac{t^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}\right)\cdot\mathrm{\Phi}\left({\sigma t}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, from (
A.6
) and (
A.5
), we further have that

(A.8)

\displaystyle\Pr\left[X>a\right]\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\cdot X)]}{\exp(t\cdot a)}=\exp\left(N\cdot\frac{t^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}-t\cdot a+N\cdot\log\left[2\cdot\mathrm{\Phi}\left(\sigma t\right)\right]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

As we prove in Lemma
A.4
, for every
x\geq 0
, we have

\mathrm{\Phi}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\Pr\left[X>a\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(N\cdot t^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}/2-t\cdot a+N\cdot\log\left[1+\sigma\cdot t\cdot\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(N\cdot{t^{2}\cdot\sigma^{2}}/{2}-t\cdot a+N\cdot\sigma\cdot t\cdot\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}\right)

(A.9)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(N\left[t^{2}\cdot{\sigma^{2}}/{2}+t\cdot\left(\sigma\cdot\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}-{a}/{N}\right)\right]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Minimizing the exponent in the rhs of (
A.9
) with respect to
t\geq 0
, yields that for every

(A.10)

\textstyle a\geq N\sigma\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\kern 5.0pt,

we have

(A.11)

\Pr\left[X>a\right]\leq\exp\left(N\left[-\frac{\left(a\sqrt{\pi}-\sigma N\sqrt{2}\right)^{2}}{2\pi N^{2}\sigma^{2}}\right]\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Considering the derivative at
t=0
of the moment generating function of
|X_{1}|
in (
A.7
), it is easy to check that

\mathbb{E}[X]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{N}|X_{i}|\right]=N\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{1}|\right]=N\cdot\sigma\cdot\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}\kern 5.0pt.

So that condition (
A.10
), can be written as
a\geq\mathbb{E}[X]
, or equivalently,
a=(1+\delta)\cdot\mathbb{E}[X]
for some
\delta\geq 0
.
Substituting
a=(1+\delta)\cdot\mathbb{E}[X]
in (
A.11
), gives (
A.4
), concluding the
proof.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\mathrm{\Phi}:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
be the CDF of the Standard Gaussian distribution. Then, for every
x\geq 0

\mathrm{\Phi}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

Define
H:[0,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
by
H(x)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{x}{\sqrt{2\pi}}-\mathrm{\Phi}(x)
.
Differentiating gives

\displaystyle H^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}-\frac{\exp(-\frac{x^{2}}{2})}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\geq 0\kern 5.0pt,

so that
H
is increasing. Noticing also that
H(0)=0
, yields
H\geq 0
, as desired.
∎

A.5.
Proof of
Proposition

13.3

To simplify the notation of this proof, we consider a general setting, i.e., rather than
the blocks
B\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

Consider graph
G=(V_{n},E)
and Gibbs distribution
\mu:\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}}\to[0,1]
.
Let
\uptau_{\rm relax}
be the relaxation time of discrete–time Glauber dynamics
on
\mu
, while let
{C}_{\rm mLSI}
be the modified log-Sobolev.

Proposition

13.3
follows by showing that

(A.12)

\displaystyle{C}_{\rm mLSI}\geq\left(-\log(\mu_{\min})\cdot\tau_{\rm relax}\right)^{-1}\kern 5.0pt,

where
\mu_{\min}=\min\{\mu(\sigma)\ |\ \sigma\in\{\pm 1\}^{V},\ \mu(\sigma)>0\}
.

The proof is standard, but we need to introduce a few concepts.
Let function
f:\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. For set
S\subset V_{n}
and

\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}\setminus S}
.

\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}\setminus S}
.
Define the function
f_{\tau}:\{\pm 1\}^{S}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
by having

f_{\tau}(\sigma)=f(\tau\cup\sigma)
for all
\sigma\in\{\pm 1\}^{S}
.

Let
\mathrm{Ent}_{S}^{\tau}(f_{\tau})
denote the entropy of
f_{\tau}
with respect to the conditional
distribution
\mu^{\tau}_{S}
. Furthermore, we let

\displaystyle\mu(\mathrm{Ent}_{S}(f))

\displaystyle=\sum\nolimits_{\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{V\setminus S}}\mu_{V\setminus S}(\tau)\cdot\mathrm{Ent}_{S}^{\tau}(f_{\tau})\kern 5.0pt.

When
S=\{v\}
, i.e., the set
S
is a singleton, we abbreviate
\mu(\mathrm{Ent}_{\{v\}}(f))
to

\mu(\mathrm{Ent}_{v}(f))
.

For distribution
\mu
with support
\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}}
satisfies the
approximate
tensorisation
of entropy with constant
C_{\rm Tens}>0
if for all
f:\{\pm 1\}^{V_{n}}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ent}_{\mu}(f)\leq C_{\rm Tens}\cdot\sum\nolimits_{u\in V}\mu\left(\mathrm{Ent}_{u}(f)\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Using
[
10
, Proposition 1.1]
and
[
18
, Corollary A.4]

and standard derivations, obtain that

(A.13)

\displaystyle C_{\rm Tens}\leq\frac{-\log(\mu_{\min})}{|V_{n}|}\cdot\tau_{\rm relax}\kern 5.0pt.

From
[
14
, Fact 3.5]
, we have that if
\mu
satisfies the approximate tensorisation
with constant
C_{\rm Tens}
, then it satisfies the modified log-Sobolev inequality for

(A.14)

\displaystyle{C}_{\rm mLSI}=\left(|V_{n}|\cdot C_{\rm Tens}\right)^{-1}\kern 5.0pt.

Eq. (
A.12
) follows by plugging (
A.13
)
into (
A.14
).
All the above conclude the proof of
Proposition

13.3
.
